    2. Qxc4
    Now Black has a choice.
    Maybe `b8d7` or `c8e6` to develop.
    Black is up a Bishop for a Pawn. This is a significant material advantage.

    Is there any other move?
    `e4c4` (Queen takes Bishop)
    2. Qxc4 (Queen takes Queen)
    This is a bad trade for Black.

    What about `d5d4`?
    It attacks c3.
    If `d5d4`, White might play `Na4` or `Nb1`.
    But `d5c4` simply wins a piece.

    Wait, let me double-check the board one more time.
    Bishop at c4.
    Pawn at d5.
    Queen at b3.
    Is it possible that `d5c4` is a mistake?
    If `d5c4`, White plays `Qxc4`.
    Is there any other piece that can recapture?
    The knight on c3? No, it's at c3. The knight at e2? No.
    The pawn at b2? No.
    So only `Qxc4`.

    Wait, what if White doesn't recapture?
    If White doesn't recapture, Black just won a bishop for free.

    So `d5c4` seems very strong.
    Is there any other move that is better?
    `e4c4` is bad.
    `d5d4` is slow.
    `c6c5` is slow.
    Capturing the bishop seems like the most direct way to gain an advantage.

    Final check on `d5c4`:
    - Black Pawn d5 takes White Bishop c4.
    - White Queen b3 takes Black Pawn c4.
    - Result: Material gain for Black (Bishop for Pawn).
    - Position: Black's Queen remains centrally located. Black's king is still in the center, but White's king is also in the center.

    Wait, let's check if `d5c4` opens any dangerous lines.
    If `d5c4`, the pawn moves from d5 to c4.
    The d-file is now open.
    Does it help White?
    White has no pieces on the d-file.
    Does it help Black?
    Black has no pieces on the d-file, but the Queen is at e4.

    Is there any other threat?
    White's Queen is at b3.
    White's Knight is at c3.
    White's Knight is at e2.
    White's Bishop is at c1.
    White's Rook is at a1.
    White's King is at e1.

    One more thing: is there any move that puts the King in check?
    `e4e2` (Queen takes Knight) - `Kxe2`.
    `e4f3` (Queen attacks f3, not a check).
    `e4g2` (Queen takes pawn) - `Kxg2` or `Rxg2`.

    `d5c4` looks like the clear winner.

    Wait, let me just re-read the coordinates one more time.
    Row 5: ⭘ ⭘ ⭘ ♟ ⭘ ⭘ ⭘ ⭘

    Row 3, col 2 (b3) is ♕ (White Queen).
